· GitHub","url":"https://github.com/SWE-bench/SWE-bench"},{"title":"Introducing SWE-bench Verified | OpenAI","url":"https://openai.com/index/introducing-swe-bench-verified/"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified | Epoch AI","url":"https://epoch.ai/benchmarks/swe-bench-verified"},{"title":"Evaluation - SWE-bench","url":"https://www.swebench.com/SWE-bench/guides/evaluation/"}]},"Based on the search results, I found information about SWE-bench's per-test evaluation and the test categories:\n\n## PASS_TO_PASS and FAIL_TO_PASS Test Categories\n\nThe SWE-bench dataset comprises 300 GitHub issues with repository snapshots, natural-language bug descriptions, gold patches, and categorized unit tests (PASS_TO_PASS and FAIL_TO_PASS).\n\nThe two test categories have distinct purposes:\n\n- **FAIL_TO_PASS**: Tests which fail on the buggy code, but pass after applying the correct patch.\n\n- **PASS_TO_PASS**: Tests which must pass both before and after patching.\n\n## Evaluation Methodology\n\nSWE-bench evaluates models by applying their generated patches to real-world repositories and running the repository's tests to verify if the issue is resolved, performed in a containerized Docker environment to ensure consistent results across different platforms.\n\nThe per-test evaluation distinguishes between the two test categories to ensure that solutions not only fix the identified issue (FAIL_TO_PASS tests) but also maintain existing functionality (PASS_TO_PASS tests). SWE-RM: Execution-free Feedback For Software Engineering Agents\",\"url\":\"https://huggingface.co/papers/2512.21919\"},{\"title\":\"[2512.17419] SWE-Bench++: A Framework for the Scalable Generation of Software Engineering Benchmarks from Open-Source Repositories\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17419\"},{\"title\":\"[2310.06770] SWE-bench: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World GitHub Issues?\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.06770\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench++: A Framework for the Scalable Generation of Software Engineering Benchmarks from Open-Source Repositories\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2512.17419v1\"},{\"title\":\"Does SWE-Bench-Verified Test Agent Ability or Model Memory?\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2512.10218\"},{\"title\":\"[2512.10218] Does SWE-Bench-Verified Test Agent Ability or Model Memory?\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10218\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found information about the arXiv paper 2512.21919 titled \"SWE-RM: Execution-free Feedback For Software Engineering Agents.\"\n\nThe paper discusses execution-based feedback like unit testing widely used in the development of coding agents through test-time scaling (TTS) and reinforcement learning (RL). The traditional paradigm requires scalable and reliable collection of unit test cases to provide accurate feedback, and the resulting feedback is often sparse and cannot effectively distinguish between trajectories that are both successful or both unsuccessful.\n\nIn contrast, execution-free feedback from reward models can provide more fine-grained signals without depending on unit test cases. The paper introduces SWE-RM, an accurate and robust reward model adopting a mixture-of-experts architecture with 30B total parameters and 3B activated during inference.\n\nSWE-RM substantially improves SWE agents on both TTS and RL performance, for example increasing the accuracy of Qwen3-Coder-Flash from 51.6% to 62.0%, and Qwen3-Coder-Max from 67.0% to 74.6% on SWE-Bench Verified using TTS.\n\nHowever, the search results do not contain specific details about \"per-test evaluation pass-to-pass\" metrics mentioned in your query. You would need to access the full paper to find those specific evaluation details.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"efffb7ae-a82f-4b7d-88ef-778c105d2f16","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:43:49.985Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"SWE-RM arXiv 2512.21919 per-test evaluation pass-to-pass","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_0153KuM1u8SC2Z9rW4dsZCcV","content":[{"title":"[2512.21919] SWE-RM: Execution-free Feedback For Software Engineering Agents","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21919"},{"title":"SWE-RM: Execution-Free Feedback for Software Engineering Agents","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2512.21919"},{"title":"2025-12-29 SWE-RM: EXECUTION-FREE FEEDBACK FOR SOFT- WARE ENGINEERING AGENTS","url":"https://www.arxiv.org/pdf/2512.21919"},{"title":"SWE-RM: Execution-free Feedback For Software Engineering Agents | alphaXiv","url":"https://www.alphaxiv.org/overview/2512.21919v1"},{"title":"Paper page - SWE-RM: Execution-free Feedback For Software Engineering Agents","url":"https://huggingface.co/papers/2512.21919"},{"title":"[2512.17419] SWE-Bench++: A Framework for the Scalable Generation of Software Engineering Benchmarks from Open-Source Repositories","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17419"},{"title":"[2310.06770] SWE-bench: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World GitHub Issues?","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.06770"},{"title":"SWE-Bench++: A Framework for the Scalable Generation of Software Engineering Benchmarks from Open-Source Repositories","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2512.17419v1"},{"title":"Does SWE-Bench-Verified Test Agent Ability or Model Memory?","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2512.10218"},{"title":"[2512.10218] Does SWE-Bench-Verified Test Agent Ability or Model Memory?","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10218"}]},"Based on the search results, I found information about the arXiv paper 2512.21919 titled \"SWE-RM: Execution-free Feedback For Software Engineering Agents.\"\n\nThe paper discusses execution-based feedback like unit testing widely used in the development of coding agents through test-time scaling (TTS) and reinforcement learning (RL). The traditional paradigm requires scalable and reliable collection of unit test cases to provide accurate feedback, and the resulting feedback is often sparse and cannot effectively distinguish between trajectories that are both successful or both unsuccessful.\n\nIn contrast, execution-free feedback from reward models can provide more fine-grained signals without depending on unit test cases. The paper introduces SWE-RM, an accurate and robust reward model adopting a mixture-of-experts architecture with 30B total parameters and 3B activated during inference.\n\nSWE-RM substantially improves SWE agents on both TTS and RL performance, for example increasing the accuracy of Qwen3-Coder-Flash from 51.6% to 62.0%, and Qwen3-Coder-Max from 67.0% to 74.6% on SWE-Bench Verified using TTS.\n\nHowever, the search results do not contain specific details about \"per-test evaluation pass-to-pass\" metrics mentioned in your query. You would need to access the full paper to find those specific evaluation details."],"durationSeconds":7.571221394}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SWGNgWUeNVs2J7JxuHMctt","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"SWE-bench pass-to-pass test failure rate incorrect patch regression\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Are “Solved Issues” in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2503.15223v1\"},{\"title\":\"What skills does SWE-bench Verified evaluate? | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/blog/what-skills-does-swe-bench-verified-evaluate\"},{\"title\":\"Introducing SWE-bench Verified | OpenAI\",\"url\":\"https://openai.com/index/introducing-swe-bench-verified/\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Lite: Bug-Fixing Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-lite-dataset\"},{\"title\":\"[2503.15223] Are \\\"Solved Issues\\\" in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15223\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Verified Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-47773414-8319-4e96-b867-a5a13ef278a7\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench: A Comprehensive Review of its Fundamentals, Methodology, Impact, and Future Directions\",\"url\":\"https://atoms.dev/insights/swe-bench-a-comprehensive-review-of-its-fundamentals-methodology-impact-and-future-directions/6c3cb9820d3b44e69862f7b064c1fd1e\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench Verified Issues Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-issues\"},{\"title\":\"Cognition | SWE-bench technical report\",\"url\":\"https://cognition.ai/blog/swe-bench-technical-report\"},{\"title\":\"[Dataset & Code Fix] Resolve gold patch validation failures caused by environment and dependency mismatches (Astropy & PSF Requests) · Issue #484 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/SWE-bench/SWE-bench/issues/484\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found substantial information about SWE-bench's pass-to-pass test failures, incorrect patches, and regression issues:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n### Incorrect Patches and Test Failures\n\nAmong plausible patches generated by three issue-solving tools, 7.2% to 8.4% are functionally incorrect when subjected to all developer tests, translating to an absolute drop of 3.8% to 5.2% in reported resolution rates.\n\n### Pass-to-Pass Regression Issues\n\nSome patches generated by models lead to regressions when running the whole test suite, as SWE-bench Verified only uses the tests in the file(s) of the test patch. Additionally, test suites often run only tests modified in the original pull request rather than all available tests, which can overstate passing rates by 4–7% by missing potential regression cases.\n\n### Behavioral Divergence in Patches\n\nCritical weaknesses in SWE-bench's patch validation mechanism cause 7.8% of all patches to count as correct while failing the developer-written test suite, and 29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than the ground truth patches. Manual inspection shows that 28.6% of behaviorally divergent patches are certainly incorrect.\n\n### Overall Impact on Resolution Rates\n\nThe different weaknesses lead to an inflation of reported resolution rates by 6.2 absolute percent points.\n\n### Weak Test Suites\n\n12.5%–22% of successful patches were logically wrong or incomplete but unflagged due to insufficient tests.\n\nThese findings highlight significant limitations in SWE-bench's current evaluation methodology, particularly regarding the completeness of test coverage and the validation of patch correctness.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"aa0616eb-d464-4ebf-9db6-65cda4666d78","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:43:50.445Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"SWE-bench pass-to-pass test failure rate incorrect patch regression","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01PusxnkaF7LCG8dEmAb1aXD","content":[{"title":"Are “Solved Issues” in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2503.15223v1"},{"title":"What skills does SWE-bench Verified evaluate? | Epoch AI","url":"https://epoch.ai/blog/what-skills-does-swe-bench-verified-evaluate"},{"title":"Introducing SWE-bench Verified | OpenAI","url":"https://openai.com/index/introducing-swe-bench-verified/"},{"title":"SWE-Bench Lite: Bug-Fixing Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-lite-dataset"},{"title":"[2503.15223] Are \"Solved Issues\" in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15223"},{"title":"SWE-Bench Verified Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-47773414-8319-4e96-b867-a5a13ef278a7"},{"title":"SWE-bench: A Comprehensive Review of its Fundamentals, Methodology, Impact, and Future Directions","url":"https://atoms.dev/insights/swe-bench-a-comprehensive-review-of-its-fundamentals-methodology-impact-and-future-directions/6c3cb9820d3b44e69862f7b064c1fd1e"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified Issues Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-issues"},{"title":"Cognition | SWE-bench technical report","url":"https://cognition.ai/blog/swe-bench-technical-report"},{"title":"[Dataset & Code Fix] Resolve gold patch validation failures caused by environment and dependency mismatches (Astropy & PSF Requests) · Issue #484 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench","url":"https://github.com/SWE-bench/SWE-bench/issues/484"}]},"Based on the search results, I found substantial information about SWE-bench's pass-to-pass test failures, incorrect patches, and regression issues:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n### Incorrect Patches and Test Failures\n\nAmong plausible patches generated by three issue-solving tools, 7.2% to 8.4% are functionally incorrect when subjected to all developer tests, translating to an absolute drop of 3.8% to 5.2% in reported resolution rates.\n\n### Pass-to-Pass Regression Issues\n\nSome patches generated by models lead to regressions when running the whole test suite, as SWE-bench Verified only uses the tests in the file(s) of the test patch. Additionally, test suites often run only tests modified in the original pull request rather than all available tests, which can overstate passing rates by 4–7% by missing potential regression cases.\n\n### Behavioral Divergence in Patches\n\nCritical weaknesses in SWE-bench's patch validation mechanism cause 7.8% of all patches to count as correct while failing the developer-written test suite, and 29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than the ground truth patches. Manual inspection shows that 28.6% of behaviorally divergent patches are certainly incorrect.\n\n### Overall Impact on Resolution Rates\n\nThe different weaknesses lead to an inflation of reported resolution rates by 6.2 absolute percent points.\n\n### Weak Test Suites\n\n12.5%–22% of successful patches were logically wrong or incomplete but unflagged due to insufficient tests.\n\nThese findings highlight significant limitations in SWE-bench's current evaluation methodology, particularly regarding the completeness of test coverage and the validation of patch correctness."],"durationSeconds":8.031563380000003}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VfWPuepERuwiUWhVu7nQ1t","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"\"scaling test-time compute\" SWE-bench per-test pass-to-pass failure regression 2604.16529\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"[2604.16529] Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16529\"},{\"title\":\"Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/papers/2604.16529\"},{\"title\":\"[Paper Note] Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding, Joongwon Kim+, arXiv'26, 2026.04 · Issue #5307 · AkihikoWatanabe/paper_notes\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/AkihikoWatanabe/paper_notes/issues/5307\"},{\"title\":\"Paper page - Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding\",\"url\":\"https://huggingface.co/papers/2604.16529\"},{\"title\":\"Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding - ArXivIQ\",\"url\":\"https://arxiviq.substack.com/p/scaling-test-time-compute-for-agentic\"},{\"title\":\"Thinking Longer, Not Larger: Enhancing Software Engineering Agents via Scaling Test-Time Compute\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2503.23803v1\"},{\"title\":\"PASS_TO_PASS and FAIL_TO_PASS test cases · Issue #257 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/swe-bench/SWE-bench/issues/257\"},{\"title\":\"TEX: Test-Time Scaling Testing Agents via Execution-based Cross-Validation - Salesforce\",\"url\":\"https://www.salesforce.com/blog/tex-test-time-scaling/?bc=OTH\"},{\"title\":\"[2601.22129] SWE-Replay: Efficient Test-Time Scaling for Software Engineering Agents\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22129\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Pro: Can AI Agents Solve Long-Horizon Software Engineering Tasks?\",\"url\":\"https://static.scale.com/uploads/654197dc94d34f66c0f5184e/SWEAP_Eval_Scale%20(9).pdf\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found the paper you're looking for. Here's what I found about the arXiv paper 2604.16529:\n\n## \"Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding\"\n\nThis paper proposes a test-time scaling framework for agentic coding based on compact representations of rollout trajectories.\n\n### Key Concepts:\n\nLong-horizon coding agents produce extended trajectories of actions, observations, errors, and partial progress, and the main challenge is representing prior experience in a form that can be effectively selected from and reused.\n\n### Methods:\n\nThe framework introduces Recursive Tournament Voting (RTV), which recursively narrows a population of rollout summaries through small-group comparisons for parallel scaling. For sequential scaling, it adapts Parallel-Distill-Refine (PDR) to the agentic setting by conditioning new rollouts on summaries distilled from prior attempts.\n\n### Results on SWE-Bench:\n\nClaude-4.5-Opus improves from 70.9% to 77.6% on SWE-Bench Verified (mini-SWE-agent) and 46.9% to 59.1% on Terminal-Bench v2.0 (Terminus 1). Standalone RTV improves pass@1 scores by 5-6% on SWE-Bench Verified and 8-12% on Terminal-Bench v2.0 across model families.\n\nThe paper was published in April 2026 and addresses the fundamental challenge of effectively representing and reusing experience from multiple coding attempts to improve agent performance.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"d6579959-3606-4789-9a72-aa028901b607","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:44:18.683Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"\"scaling test-time compute\" SWE-bench per-test pass-to-pass failure regression 2604.16529","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_012hdDBeCd2khpMYgZZhRhjN","content":[{"title":"[2604.16529] Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16529"},{"title":"Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/papers/2604.16529"},{"title":"[Paper Note] Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding, Joongwon Kim+, arXiv'26, 2026.04 · Issue #5307 · AkihikoWatanabe/paper_notes","url":"https://github.com/AkihikoWatanabe/paper_notes/issues/5307"},{"title":"Paper page - Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding","url":"https://huggingface.co/papers/2604.16529"},{"title":"Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding - ArXivIQ","url":"https://arxiviq.substack.com/p/scaling-test-time-compute-for-agentic"},{"title":"Thinking Longer, Not Larger: Enhancing Software Engineering Agents via Scaling Test-Time Compute","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2503.23803v1"},{"title":"PASS_TO_PASS and FAIL_TO_PASS test cases · Issue #257 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench","url":"https://github.com/swe-bench/SWE-bench/issues/257"},{"title":"TEX: Test-Time Scaling Testing Agents via Execution-based Cross-Validation - Here's what I found about the arXiv paper 2604.16529:\n\n## \"Scaling Test-Time Compute for Agentic Coding\"\n\nThis paper proposes a test-time scaling framework for agentic coding based on compact representations of rollout trajectories.\n\n### Key Concepts:\n\nLong-horizon coding agents produce extended trajectories of actions, observations, errors, and partial progress, and the main challenge is representing prior experience in a form that can be effectively selected from and reused.\n\n### Methods:\n\nThe framework introduces Recursive Tournament Voting (RTV), which recursively narrows a population of rollout summaries through small-group comparisons for parallel scaling. For sequential scaling, it adapts Parallel-Distill-Refine (PDR) to the agentic setting by conditioning new rollouts on summaries distilled from prior attempts.\n\n### Results on SWE-Bench:\n\nClaude-4.5-Opus improves from 70.9% to 77.6% on SWE-Bench Verified (mini-SWE-agent) and 46.9% to 59.1% on Terminal-Bench v2.0 (Terminus 1). Standalone RTV improves pass@1 scores by 5-6% on SWE-Bench Verified and 8-12% on Terminal-Bench v2.0 across model families.\n\nThe paper was published in April 2026 and addresses the fundamental challenge of effectively representing and reusing experience from multiple coding attempts to improve agent performance."],"durationSeconds":7.189088205}}

Here are the key findings from the paper \"Are 'Solved Issues' in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study\":\n\n**Full Test Suite Failure Results:**\n7.8% of all patches count as correct while failing the developer-written test suite.\n\n**Behavioral Discrepancies (Pass-to-Pass Breakdown):**\n29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than the ground truth patches.\n\n**Reasons for Behavioral Differences:**\nThese behavioral differences are often due to similar, but divergent implementations (46.8%) and due to generated patches that adapt more behavior than the ground truth patches (27.3%).\n\n**Manual Inspection Results:**\n28.6% of behaviorally divergent patches are certainly incorrect.\n\n**Overall Impact:**\nThe different weaknesses lead to an inflation of reported resolution rates by 6.2 absolute percent points.\n\nThe study examined patches from three state-of-the-art issue-solving tools (CodeStory, LearnByInteract, and OpenHands) evaluated on SWE-bench Verified, using a novel technique called PatchDiff for differential patch testing.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"88d44d14-4237-4580-81e0-91347134b652","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:44:36.563Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"arxiv 2503.15223 \"Solved Issues\" SWE-bench full test suite failure pass-to-pass breakdown percentage tests failed","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01ENbzgPSWPpWcuFhydPJjwM","content":[{"title":"[2503.15223] Are \"Solved Issues\" in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? Here are the key findings from the paper \"Are 'Solved Issues' in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study\":\n\n**Full Test Suite Failure Results:**\n7.8% of all patches count as correct while failing the developer-written test suite.\n\n**Behavioral Discrepancies (Pass-to-Pass Breakdown):**\n29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than the ground truth patches.\n\n**Reasons for Behavioral Differences:**\nThese behavioral differences are often due to similar, but divergent implementations (46.8%) and due to generated patches that adapt more behavior than the ground truth patches (27.3%).\n\n**Manual Inspection Results:**\n28.6% of behaviorally divergent patches are certainly incorrect.\n\n**Overall Impact:**\nThe different weaknesses lead to an inflation of reported resolution rates by 6.2 absolute percent points.\n\nThe study examined patches from three state-of-the-art issue-solving tools (CodeStory, LearnByInteract, and OpenHands) evaluated on SWE-bench Verified, using a novel technique called PatchDiff for differential patch testing."],"durationSeconds":6.366671331999998}}

An Empirical Study\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2503.15223v1\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench++: A Framework for the Scalable Generation of Software Engineering Benchmarks from Open-Source Repositories\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2512.17419v1\"},{\"title\":\"TDD-Bench Verified: Can LLMs Generate Tests for Issues Before They Get Resolved?\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2412.02883v1\"},{\"title\":\"Dissecting the SWE-Bench Leaderboards: Profiling Submitters and Architectures of LLM- and Agent-Based Repair Systems\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.17208v2\"},{\"title\":\"The SWE-Bench Illusion: When State-of-the-Art LLMs Remember Instead of Reason\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.12286v4\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-EVO: Benchmarking Coding Agents in Long-Horizon Software Evolution Scenarios\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2512.18470v2\"},{\"title\":\"UTBoost: Rigorous Evaluation of Coding Agents on SWE-Bench\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.09289v1\"}]\n\nI found the arxiv paper 2510.18270 titled \"When Old Meets New: Evaluating the Impact of Regression Tests on SWE Issue Resolution.\" Here's what the search results reveal about regression tests and pass-to-pass failure rate statistics:\n\n## Overview\n\nThe purpose of regression tests is to ensure the code that worked before keeps working: they check that the correct existing behavior does not regress.\n\n## Pass-to-Pass Test Context\n\nIn the SWE-Bench evaluation framework, the generated patch is evaluated by running tests associated with the issue, including at least one fail-to-pass test to confirm the issue is resolved, and, where available, several pass-to-pass tests to ensure no regression in existing functionality.\n\n## TestPrune Performance\n\nThe paper introduces TestPrune, which achieves significant improvements. TestPrune demonstrates its ability to select a minimized subset of relevant tests to the issues of SWE-Bench Verified and SWE-Bench Lite problems, reducing the size by over 0.63 and coverage recall (the fraction of buggy lines covered by TestPrune regression tests relative to the lines covered by the whole test suite) of 0.71.\n\nAdditionally, providing tests selected by TestPrune to Otter, an agentic reproduction test generation approach, compared to original regression tests, yielded a 6.2%-9.0% relative improvement in issue reproduction rate on SWE-Bench Lite and SWE-Bench Verified benchmarks.\n\nHowever, the search results don't contain specific pass-to-pass failure rate statistics as a standalone metric. If you need more detailed statistical breakdowns of pass-to-pass test failures, you may want to consult the full PDF of the paper directly.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"67e75e5b-b451-4071-8894-b011e922b2c8","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:44:37.120Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"arxiv 2510.18270 \"regression tests\" SWE-bench pass-to-pass failure rate statistics","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01VoGP83o9oG1g8zxUxyHRP1","content":[{"title":"When Old Meets New: Evaluating the Impact of Regression Tests on SWE Issue Resolution","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2510.18270"},{"title":"[2510.18270] When Old Meets New: Evaluating the Impact of Regression Tests on SWE Issue Resolution","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18270"},{"title":"Evaluating the Impact of Regression Tests on SWE Issue ...","url":"https://www.arxiv.org/pdf/2510.18270"},{"title":"Are “Solved Issues” in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? TestPrune demonstrates its ability to select a minimized subset of relevant tests to the issues of SWE-Bench Verified and SWE-Bench Lite problems, reducing the size by over 0.63 and coverage recall (the fraction of buggy lines covered by TestPrune regression tests relative to the lines covered by the whole test suite) of 0.71.\n\nAdditionally, providing tests selected by TestPrune to Otter, an agentic reproduction test generation approach, compared to original regression tests, yielded a 6.2%-9.0% relative improvement in issue reproduction rate on SWE-Bench Lite and SWE-Bench Verified benchmarks.\n\nHowever, the search results don't contain specific pass-to-pass failure rate statistics as a standalone metric. Here's what I discovered:\n\n## SWE-bench Evaluation Harness report.json Format\n\nThe evaluation results are stored in the evaluation_results directory, with subdirectories for each run.\n\n### Per-Test Results Structure\n\nThe report.json file contains per-instance evaluation results with the following structure:\n\nFor each instance, the report includes fields like \"patch_is_None\", \"patch_exists\", \"patch_successfully_applied\", \"resolved\", and \"tests_status\".\n\n### Test Status Details\n\nThe tests_status object contains two main test categories - \"FAIL_TO_PASS\" and \"PASS_TO_PASS\" - each with \"success\" and \"failure\" arrays listing which tests passed and failed respectively.\n\nThe format follows this pattern:\n```json\n{\n  \"instance_id\": {\n    \"patch_is_None\": boolean,\n    \"patch_exists\": boolean,\n    \"patch_successfully_applied\": boolean,\n    \"resolved\": boolean,\n    \"tests_status\": {\n      \"FAIL_TO_PASS\": {\n        \"success\": [test_names],\n        \"failure\": [test_names]\n      },\n      \"PASS_TO_PASS\": {\n        \"success\": [test_names],\n        \"failure\": [test_names]\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Running the Evaluation\n\nThe main entry point for evaluation is the swebench.harness.run_evaluation module. You can invoke it with:\n\n```bash\npython -m swebench.harness.run_evaluation \\\n  --dataset_name princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Lite \\\n  --predictions_path &lt;path_to_predictions> \\\n  --max_workers 8 \\\n  --run_id my_evaluation_run\n```\n\nThis will generate detailed per-test pass/fail results for each instance in the report.json output.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"1340912b-bcc7-449a-a8e3-4dcef1dbfce2","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:44:42.347Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"SWE-bench evaluation harness report.json per-test pass fail results format output","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_014DEpZCR7sMzXtn6Rw9tHid","content":[{"title":"Evaluation - · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/SWE-bench/SWE-bench\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - multi-swe-bench/experiments\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/multi-swe-bench/experiments\"},{\"title\":\"SWE Bench Evaluation · ruvnet/claude-flow Wiki\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/ruvnet/claude-flow/wiki/SWE-Bench-Evaluation\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/swe-bench\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench\",\"url\":\"https://www.swebench.com/original.html\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - SWE-bench/sb-cli: Run SWE-bench evaluations remotely\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/SWE-bench/sb-cli\"},{\"title\":\"SWE Bench Evaluation · ruvnet/ruflo Wiki\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo/wiki/SWE-Bench-Evaluation\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - multi-swe-bench/multi-swe-bench: Multi-SWE-bench: A Multilingual Benchmark for Issue Resolving · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/multi-swe-bench/multi-swe-bench\"},{\"title\":\"experiments/checklist.md at main · SWE-bench/experiments\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/swe-bench/experiments/blob/main/checklist.md\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, I found relevant information about SWE-bench experiments evaluation results and the report.json format:\n\n## Key Findings\n\nThe logs are publicly accessible and meant to enable greater reproducibility and transparency of the experiments conducted on the SWE-bench task.\n\n### Report.json Structure\n\nThe evaluation artifacts include a `report.json` file that contains a summary of evaluation outcomes for each instance within the logs folder structure.\n\n### PASS_TO_PASS and Test Types\n\nYour search query references \"PASS_TO_PASS,\" which appears in the submission guidelines. Submissions must not use SWE-bench test knowledge including PASS_TO_PASS and FAIL_TO_PASS. The primary evaluation signal is the \"Fail-to-Pass\" tests - these \"Fail-to-Pass\" tests are the primary signal for evaluation.\n\n### Accessing Results\n\nYou can download the logs and trajectories for each submission by running a Python command to download the data from a public S3 Bucket. To get results, you can run `python -m analysis.get_results evaluation/&lt;split>/&lt;date + model>`.\n\nThe evaluation results structure includes individual test outcomes tracked in the report.json files within the evaluation artifact directories. evaluation data:\n\n## SWE-bench Experiments Repository Overview\n\nThe SWE-bench/experiments repository contains open sourced predictions, execution logs, trajectories, and results from model inference and evaluation runs on the SWE-bench task.\n\n## Evaluation Structure\n\nThe repository consists of repo-level subfolders organized by test split repositories, for example pallets/flask is a test split repository with a flask/ folder under validation/test/.\n\nThe validation/test_202404 folder contains a re-run of validation performed in April 2024 to ensure reproducibility of task instances' behavior since SWE-bench was created in September 2023.\n\n## Accessing the Data\n\nThese logs are publicly accessible and meant to enable greater reproducibility and transparency of the experiments conducted on the SWE-bench task.\n\nYou can download the logs and trajectories for each submission by running a command to download the data from the repository, with logs and trajectories saved to a public S3 Bucket.\n\n## Submission Requirements\n\nThe SWE-bench team plans to only accept submissions with reasoning traces for the SWE-bench leaderboard. This requirement helps balance transparency with the needs of researchers who may not want to release proprietary systems.\n\nThe repository provides detailed per-test pass/fail data through execution logs and maintains transparency in evaluating AI systems' ability to resolve GitHub issues.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"e6058751-62bf-4a5b-93d7-23d37dbb9b16","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:45:18.952Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"SWE-bench test split repository with a flask/ folder under validation/test/.\n\nThe validation/test_202404 folder contains a re-run of validation performed in April 2024 to ensure reproducibility of task {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TyG7bSkVqUgYP7G6BnaUDh","type":"tool_result","content":"# Analysis of Pass-to-Pass Test Statistics\n\nAfter thoroughly reviewing this academic paper on TestPrune, I found **no specific data about pass-to-pass (P2P) test failure rates, percentages of patches causing P2P regressions, or distributions of P2P test failures per patch**.\n\n## What the Paper Actually Contains\n\nThe research focuses on **fail-to-pass (F→P) metrics** for reproduction tests, not pass-to-pass test failures. Key metrics reported include:\n\n- **F→P @ 1 rates**: Improvements ranging from 6.2%–9.0% with TestPrune\n- **Issue resolution rates**: Increases of 9.4%–12.9% when combining TestPrune with reproduction tests\n- **Test suite reduction**: Over 1,000× reduction from original test suites to minimized sets\n- **Regression test coverage**: Precision of 0.63 and coverage recall of 0.71\n\n## Why This Distinction Matters\n\nThe paper evaluates how well selected regression tests validate patches through **execution on generated candidates**. However, the authors do not separately report statistics specifically quantifying how many patches fail on previously-passing tests, which would constitute P2P regression data.\n\nThe closest relevant finding states that TestPrune helps \"exclude patches that break correct functionality,\" but no numerical breakdown of such failures is provided in the document."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"4b29967a-e91b-4a96-8a1c-6f8585149cf0","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:46:31.415Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":261540,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Analysis of Pass-to-Pass Test Statistics\n\nAfter thoroughly reviewing this academic paper on TestPrune, I found **no specific data about pass-to-pass (P2P) test failure rates, percentages of patches causing P2P regressions, or distributions of P2P test failures per patch**.\n\n## What the Paper Actually Contains\n\nThe research focuses on **fail-to-pass (F→P) metrics** for reproduction tests, not pass-to-pass test failures. Key metrics reported include:\n\n- **F→P @ 1 rates**: Improvements ranging from 6.2%–9.0% with TestPrune\n- **Issue resolution rates**: Increases of 9.4%–12.9% when combining TestPrune with reproduction tests\n- **Test suite reduction**: Over 1,000× reduction from original test suites to minimized sets\n- **Regression test coverage**: Precision of 0.63 and coverage recall of 0.71\n\n## Why This Distinction Matters\n\nThe paper evaluates how well selected regression tests validate patches through **execution on generated candidates**. However, the authors do not separately report statistics specifically quantifying how many patches fail on previously-passing tests, which would constitute P2P regression data.\n\nThe closest relevant finding states that TestPrune helps \"exclude patches that break correct functionality,\" but no numerical breakdown of such failures is provided in the document.","durationMs":5023,"url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2510.18270"}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01N9quRBorfiyUEYZXTE2gVc","type":"tool_result","content":"# Analysis of P2P Test Failure Statistics\n\nAfter thoroughly reviewing this academic paper, I must report that **the document does not contain the specific statistics you requested**.\n\n## What the Paper Actually Addresses\n\nThe study focuses on **plausible but incorrect patches** on SWE-bench, but it does not provide:\n\n- Pass-to-pass (P2P) test failure rates broken down by patch type\n- Distribution data (mean, median, percentiles) of test failures\n- Per-test failure breakdown tables or figures\n- Comparison between P2P failures vs F2P (fail-to-pass) failures\n\n## Key Findings Present in the Paper\n\nThe paper does report:\n\n1. **Overall incorrect patch rates**: \"7.8% of all patches count as 'correct' while failing the developer-written test suite\" (RQ1)\n\n2. **Behavioral discrepancies**: \"29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than ground truth patches\" (RQ2)\n\n3. **Manual correctness assessment**: Among 77 sampled suspicious patches, \"28.6% of suspicious patches are certainly incorrect\"\n\n4. **Total patches analyzed**: Study covers 500 tasks from SWE-bench Verified across three tools (CodeStory, LearnByInteract, OpenHands)\n\nThe research uses \"differential patch testing\" to identify behavioral discrepancies but does not segregate failures by test category type (P2P vs F2P) in the reported statistics."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"28e2122b-c81d-4e23-9cd9-b7478d9d7bae","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:46:34.195Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":202577,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Analysis of P2P Test Failure Statistics\n\nAfter thoroughly reviewing this academic paper, I must report that **the document does not contain the specific statistics you requested**.\n\n## What the Paper Actually Addresses\n\nThe study focuses on **plausible but incorrect patches** on SWE-bench, but it does not provide:\n\n- Pass-to-pass (P2P) test failure rates broken down by patch type\n- Distribution data (mean, median, percentiles) of test failures\n- Per-test failure breakdown tables or figures\n- Comparison between P2P failures vs F2P (fail-to-pass) failures\n\n## Key Findings Present in the Paper\n\nThe paper does report:\n\n1. **Overall incorrect patch rates**: \"7.8% of all patches count as 'correct' while failing the developer-written test suite\" (RQ1)\n\n2. **Behavioral discrepancies**: \"29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than ground truth patches\" (RQ2)\n\n3. **Manual correctness assessment**: Among 77 sampled suspicious patches, \"28.6% of suspicious patches are certainly incorrect\"\n\n4. **Total patches analyzed**: Study covers 500 tasks from SWE-bench Verified across three tools (CodeStory, LearnByInteract, OpenHands)\n\nThe research uses \"differential patch testing\" to identify behavioral discrepancies but does not segregate failures by test category type (P2P vs F2P) in the reported statistics.","durationMs":10264,"url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2503.15223v1"}}

An Empirical Study\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15223\"},{\"title\":\"Introducing SWE-bench Verified | OpenAI\",\"url\":\"https://openai.com/index/introducing-swe-bench-verified/\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Lite: Bug-Fixing Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-lite-dataset\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench: A Comprehensive Review of its Fundamentals, Methodology, Impact, and Future Directions\",\"url\":\"https://atoms.dev/insights/swe-bench-a-comprehensive-review-of-its-fundamentals-methodology-impact-and-future-directions/6c3cb9820d3b44e69862f7b064c1fd1e\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Pro Leaderboard AI Coding Benchmark (Public Dataset) | Scale\",\"url\":\"https://labs.scale.com/leaderboard/swe_bench_pro_public\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench Verified Issues Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-issues\"},{\"title\":\"PASS_TO_PASS tests failing on original program · Issue #68 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SWE-bench/issues/68\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench Verified Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found highly relevant research on SWE-bench patch validation issues. Here are the key findings related to your query:\n\n## Pass-to-Pass Test Failure Statistics and Incorrect Patches\n\nAmong patches generated by three state-of-the-art tools, 7.2% to 8.4% are functionally incorrect when subjected to all developer tests, translating to an absolute drop of 3.8% to 5.2% in reported resolution rates.\n\nMore comprehensively, 7.8% of all patches count as correct while failing the developer-written test suite, and 29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than ground truth patches, with 28.6% of behaviorally divergent patches being certainly incorrect, leading to an inflation of reported resolution rates by 6.2 absolute percent points.\n\n## Regression and Test Suite Issues\n\nFlawed patch validation occurs when test suites run only tests modified in the original PR rather than all available tests, overstating passing rates by 4–7% by missing potential regression cases.\n\nAbout 31% of instances with passing patches rely on insufficiently robust test suites, with weak oracles failing to catch incomplete or incorrect modifications, allowing \"plausible\" but semantically wrong patches to pass evaluation.\n\n## Broken Tests in Pass-to-Pass\n\nSome PASS_TO_PASS tests fail on the original version of the subject when no patch was applied, indicating pre-existing test infrastructure issues in the benchmark.\n\nThese findings highlight systematic weaknesses in SWE-bench's evaluation mechanisms, particularly around regression detection and the completeness of test suites used for validation.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown An Empirical Study","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15223"},{"title":"Introducing SWE-bench Verified | OpenAI","url":"https://openai.com/index/introducing-swe-bench-verified/"},{"title":"SWE-Bench Lite: Bug-Fixing Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-lite-dataset"},{"title":"SWE-bench: A Comprehensive Review of its Fundamentals, Methodology, Impact, and Future Directions","url":"https://atoms.dev/insights/swe-bench-a-comprehensive-review-of-its-fundamentals-methodology-impact-and-future-directions/6c3cb9820d3b44e69862f7b064c1fd1e"},{"title":"SWE-Bench Pro Leaderboard AI Coding Benchmark (Public Dataset) | Scale","url":"https://labs.scale.com/leaderboard/swe_bench_pro_public"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified Issues Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-issues"},{"title":"PASS_TO_PASS tests failing on original program · Issue #68 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench","url":"https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SWE-bench/issues/68"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified"}]},"Based on the search results, I found highly relevant research on SWE-bench patch validation issues. Here are the key findings related to your query:\n\n## Pass-to-Pass Test Failure Statistics and Incorrect Patches\n\nAmong patches generated by three state-of-the-art tools, 7.2% to 8.4% are functionally incorrect when subjected to all developer tests, translating to an absolute drop of 3.8% to 5.2% in reported resolution rates.\n\nMore comprehensively, 7.8% of all patches count as correct while failing the developer-written test suite, and 29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than ground truth patches, with 28.6% of behaviorally divergent patches being certainly incorrect, leading to an inflation of reported resolution rates by 6.2 absolute percent points.\n\n## Regression and Test Suite Issues\n\nFlawed patch validation occurs when test suites run only tests modified in the original PR rather than all available tests, overstating passing rates by 4–7% by missing potential regression cases.\n\nAbout 31% of instances with passing patches rely on insufficiently robust test suites, with weak oracles failing to catch incomplete or incorrect modifications, allowing \"plausible\" but semantically wrong patches to pass evaluation.\n\n## Broken Tests in Pass-to-Pass\n\nSome PASS_TO_PASS tests fail on the original version of the subject when no patch was applied, indicating pre-existing test infrastructure issues in the benchmark.\n\nThese findings highlight systematic weaknesses in SWE-bench's evaluation mechanisms, particularly around regression detection and the completeness of test suites used for validation."],"durationSeconds":7.204401027999993}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Kaqb1eiGCM6pHJdas1t6B2","type":"tool_result","content":"# UTBoost: Pass-to-Pass Test Failure Analysis\n\nBased on the document, here are the specific statistics about test failures and patch analysis:\n\n## Key Numbers on Erroneous Patches\n\n**SWE-Bench Lite:**\n- 170 erroneous patches identified (28.4% of 599 patches evaluated)\n- 64 additional erroneous patches uncovered by improved parser\n- Total: 176 erroneous patches incorrectly marked as passed\n\n**SWE-Bench Verified:**\n- 92 erroneous patches identified (15.7% of 584 patches evaluated)\n- 79 additional erroneous patches uncovered by improved parser\n- Total: 169 erroneous patches incorrectly marked as passed\n\n## Insufficient Test Cases Identified\n\nThe study identified instances with inadequate test coverage:\n- 23 instances in SWE-Bench Lite (7.7% of 300 total)\n- 26 instances in SWE-Bench Verified (5.2% of 500 total)\n\n## Parser-Related Issues\n\nThe improved parser corrected annotation errors in:\n- \"54.7% (164/300) of the instances in SWE-Bench Lite\"\n- \"54.2% (271/500) of the instances in SWE-Bench Verified\"\n\n## Leaderboard Impact\n\nThese findings resulted in ranking changes affecting:\n- 40.9% of SWE-Bench Lite entries (18 of 44 agents)\n- 24.4% of SWE-Bench Verified entries (11 of 45 agents)\n\nThe document does not provide granular per-test failure rates or complete P2P failure breakdowns by category."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"fcb16979-74d2-4907-9e63-e904e34ea965","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:46:58.982Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":420743,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# UTBoost: Pass-to-Pass Test Failure Analysis\n\nBased on the document, here are the specific statistics about test failures and patch analysis:\n\n## Key Numbers on Erroneous Patches\n\n**SWE-Bench Lite:**\n- 170 erroneous patches identified (28.4% of 599 patches evaluated)\n- 64 additional erroneous patches uncovered by improved parser\n- Total: 176 erroneous patches incorrectly marked as passed\n\n**SWE-Bench Verified:**\n- 92 erroneous patches identified (15.7% of 584 patches evaluated)\n- 79 additional erroneous patches uncovered by improved parser\n- Total: 169 erroneous patches incorrectly marked as passed\n\n## Insufficient Test Cases Identified\n\nThe study identified instances with inadequate test coverage:\n- 23 instances in SWE-Bench Lite (7.7% of 300 total)\n- 26 instances in SWE-Bench Verified (5.2% of 500 total)\n\n## Parser-Related Issues\n\nThe improved parser corrected annotation errors in:\n- \"54.7% (164/300) of the instances in SWE-Bench Lite\"\n- \"54.2% (271/500) of the instances in SWE-Bench Verified\"\n\n## Leaderboard Impact\n\nThese findings resulted in ranking changes affecting:\n- 40.9% of SWE-Bench Lite entries (18 of 44 agents)\n- 24.4% of SWE-Bench Verified entries (11 of 45 agents)\n\nThe document does not provide granular per-test failure rates or complete P2P failure breakdowns by category.","durationMs":5156,"url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.09289v1"}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TwKWYEBAW38e4uCtFZA8MU","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"=== get_eval_report ===\\\\n(test_spec: swebench.harness.test_spec.test_spec.TestSpec, prediction: dict[str, str], test_log_path: str, include_tests_status: bool) -> dict[str, typing.Any]\\\\n\\\\n=== get_logs_eval ===\\\\n(test_spec: swebench.harness.test_spec.test_spec.TestSpec, log_fp: str) -> tuple[dict[str, str], bool]\\\\ndef get_logs_eval(test_spec: TestSpec, log_fp: str) -> tuple[dict[str, str], bool]:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    Retrieve evaluation results for a task instance from its corresponding log file\\\\n\\\\n    Args:\\\\n        log_fp (str): path to log file\\\\n    Returns:\\\\n        bool: whether the patch applied successfully\\\\n        dict: status map\\\\n\\\\n    TODO(john-b-yang): Check this is working properly...\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    repo = test_spec.repo\\\\n    version = test_spec.version\\\\n    log_parser = MAP_REPO_TO_PARSER[repo]\\\\n    test_cmd = MAP_REPO_VERSION_TO_SPECS[repo][version][\\\\\\\"test_cmd\\\\\\\"]\\\\n    if isinstance(test_cmd, list):\\\\n        test_cmd = test_cmd[-1]\\\\n\\\\n    with open(log_fp) as f:\\\\n        content = f.read()\\\\n        # {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01X6J83qCEZ3r8DY9hcYRqNg","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01GfR9jz5rrM5LCsKPFThM2m","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"home-1x5090","account":"sonny","command":"cd /home/sonny/exec-sim-repair-verifier && python3 << 'PYEOF'\n# Try to download evaluation logs from the SWE-bench experiments S3 bucket\n# The log format should be at: s3://swe-bench-experiments/...\nimport subprocess, os, json\n\n# Check if we can use the swebench download util\ntry:\n    from swebench.harness.log_parsers import MAP_REPO_TO_PARSER\n    print(\"Log parsers available:\", list(MAP_REPO_TO_PARSER.keys())[:5])\nexcept ImportError:\n    print(\"No log_parsers module\")\n\n# Try fetching the README from GitHub\nresult = subprocess.run(\n    ['curl', '-sL', 'https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SWE-bench/experiments/main/README.md'],\n    capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=15\n)\nif result.returncode == 0:\n    text = result.stdout\n    # Look for S3 bucket URLs or download instructions\n    for line in text.split('\\n'):\n        if any(kw in line.lower() for kw in ['s3', 'download', 'bucket', 'aws', 'logs', 'wget', 'curl']):\n            print(line)\nPYEOF\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":344,"cache_read_input_tokens":54079,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":344},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"82cea6ad-4b10-429c-bef5-09bdf989864a"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GfR9jz5rrM5LCsKPFThM2m","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"Log parsers available: ['redis/redis', 'jqlang/jq', 'nlohmann/json', 'micropython/micropython', 'valkey-io/valkey']\\\\n│   │ ├── logs/&lt;instance_id>/&lt;exec. artifacts> (Execution Logs)\\\\n* A subfolder contains the predictions, results, execution logs, and trajectories (if applicable) for the submission.\\\\nThe `validation/` folder contains the validation logs for the dev and test splits of SWE-bench.\\\\nThese logs are publicly accessible and meant to enable greater reproducibility and transparency of the experiments conducted on the SWE-bench task.\\\\n## 🔎 Viewing Logs, Trajectories\\\\nYou can download the logs and trajectories for each submission by running the following command to download the data:\\\\npython -m analysis.download_logs evaluation/&lt;split>/&lt;date + model>\\\\npython -m analysis.download_logs evaluation/lite/20231010_rag_claude2\\\\nLogs and trajectories are saved to a public S3 Bucket. *You need an AWS account to download the logs and trajectories*. Namely, you'll need to create an [AWS account](https://aws.amazon.com/), download the [AWS CLI](https://aws.amazon.com/cli/), and [configure the CLI with your credentials](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/signin/latest/userguide/command-line-sign-in.html).\\\\n  * `logs/`: SWE-bench evaluation artifacts dump\\\\n> * You do *not* need to submit predictions, `logs/`, or `trajs/` for SWE-bench Multimodal.\\\\nFor an example, see [SWE-agent + GPT 4 Turbo Trajectories](https://github.com/swe-bench/experiments/tree/main/evaluation/lite/20240402_sweagent_gpt4/logs).\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 2.09}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"d76cfd4f-a5f2-4724-9031-d4feec8997a2","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:48:17.354Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"Log parsers available: ['redis/redis', 'jqlang/jq', 'nlohmann/json', 'micropython/micropython', 'valkey-io/valkey']\\\\n│   │ ├── logs/&lt;instance_id>/&lt;exec. artifacts> (Execution Logs)\\\\n* A subfolder contains the predictions, results, execution logs, and trajectories (if applicable) for the submission.\\\\nThe `validation/` folder contains the validation logs for the dev and test splits of SWE-bench.\\\\nThese logs are publicly accessible and meant to enable greater reproducibility and transparency of the experiments conducted on the SWE-bench task.\\\\n## 🔎 Viewing Logs, Trajectories\\\\nYou can download the logs and trajectories for each submission by running the following command to download the data:\\\\npython -m analysis.download_logs evaluation/&lt;split>/&lt;date + model>\\\\npython -m analysis.download_logs evaluation/lite/20231010_rag_claude2\\\\nLogs and trajectories are saved to a public S3 Bucket. *You need an AWS account to download the logs and trajectories*. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XUdCGmHrQvnjhUJoJzCKX7","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"SWE-bench \"test_output\" OR \"report.json\" per-test pass fail PASS_TO_PASS failure count data analysis github open source 2025\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"All the gold standards fail the test. · Issue #372 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/SWE-bench/SWE-bench/issues/372\"},{\"title\":\"Introducing SWE-bench Verified | OpenAI\",\"url\":\"https://openai.com/index/introducing-swe-bench-verified/\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench Verified Issues Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-issues\"},{\"title\":\"arXiv:2509.16941v2 [cs.SE] 14 Nov 2025 SWE-Bench Pro: Can AI Agents Solve\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.16941\"},{\"title\":\"How can I populate the FAIL_TO_PASS and PASS_TO_PASS fields? · Issue #287 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/swe-bench/SWE-bench/issues/287\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench Verified | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/benchmarks/swe-bench-verified\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench Verified\",\"url\":\"https://www.swebench.com/verified.html\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Verified Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-47773414-8319-4e96-b867-a5a13ef278a7\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Verified Benchmark Leaderboard\",\"url\":\"https://llm-stats.com/benchmarks/swe-bench-verified\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Leaderboards\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-leaderboards\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found relevant information about SWE-bench testing infrastructure and evaluation metrics in 2025. Here's what I discovered:\n\n## Key Findings on SWE-bench Testing Infrastructure\n\n### Test Categorization and Reporting\n\nSWE-Bench Verified instances include mandatory sets of FAIL_TO_PASS and PASS_TO_PASS unit tests. Every task includes human-reviewed test suites with fail2pass tests that verify issue resolution and pass2pass tests that ensure existing functionalities remain intact.\n\n### Evaluation Reports and Output Format\n\nPer-issue results are provided as JSON or tabular outputs denoting which issues are resolved, including pass/fail for each instance, along with summary files recording the core resolved count, fraction, and submission timestamp. Reports are written to JSON files (e.g., \"gold.validate-gold.json\").\n\n### Performance Metrics\n\nThe main metric is typically \"pass@1,\" i.e., the rate at which the top-ranked agent patch causes all tests to pass. Pass@1 is reported as the resolve rate.\n\n### Data Quality and Analysis\n\nRecent 2025 analysis has revealed important insights: Explicit data quality analysis reveals substantial confounds even in SWE-Bench Verified, including solution leakage (up to 33% of \"passed\" patches), and weak test suites (12.5%–22% of successful patches were logically wrong or incomplete due to insufficient tests).\n\nThe SWE-bench project is open source and available on GitHub, with active development and issue tracking throughout 2025.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"fcb4d461-26d9-4161-ac98-8812eede5ea9","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:48:58.290Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"SWE-bench \"test_output\" OR \"report.json\" per-test pass fail PASS_TO_PASS failure count data analysis github open source 2025","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_013UQRs5xjUsdpNhYKwxwE8Y","content":[{"title":"All the gold standards fail the test. · Issue #372 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench","url":"https://github.com/SWE-bench/SWE-bench/issues/372"},{"title":"Introducing SWE-bench Verified | OpenAI","url":"https://openai.com/index/introducing-swe-bench-verified/"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified Issues Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-issues"},{"title":"arXiv:2509.16941v2 [cs.SE] 14 Nov 2025 SWE-Bench Pro: Can AI Agents Solve","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.16941"},{"title":"How can I populate the FAIL_TO_PASS and PASS_TO_PASS fields? · Issue #287 · SWE-bench/SWE-bench","url":"https://github.com/swe-bench/SWE-bench/issues/287"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified | Epoch AI","url":"https://epoch.ai/benchmarks/swe-bench-verified"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified","url":"https://www.swebench.com/verified.html"},{"title":"SWE-Bench Verified Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-47773414-8319-4e96-b867-a5a13ef278a7"},{"title":"SWE-Bench Verified Benchmark Leaderboard","url":"https://llm-stats.com/benchmarks/swe-bench-verified"},{"title":"SWE-Bench Leaderboards","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-leaderboards"}]},"Based on the search results, I found relevant information about SWE-bench testing infrastructure and evaluation metrics in 2025. Here's what I discovered:\n\n## Key Findings on SWE-bench Testing Infrastructure\n\n### Test Categorization and Reporting\n\nSWE-Bench Verified instances include mandatory sets of FAIL_TO_PASS and PASS_TO_PASS unit tests. Every task includes human-reviewed test suites with fail2pass tests that verify issue resolution and pass2pass tests that ensure existing functionalities remain intact.\n\n### Evaluation Reports and Output Format\n\nPer-issue results are provided as JSON or tabular outputs denoting which issues are resolved, including pass/fail for each instance, along with summary files recording the core resolved count, fraction, and submission timestamp. Reports are written to JSON files (e.g., \"gold.validate-gold.json\").\n\n### Performance Metrics\n\nThe main metric is typically \"pass@1,\" i.e., the rate at which the top-ranked agent patch causes all tests to pass. Pass@1 is reported as the resolve rate.\n\n### Data Quality and Analysis\n\nRecent 2025 analysis has revealed important insights: Explicit data quality analysis reveals substantial confounds even in SWE-Bench Verified, including solution leakage (up to 33% of \"passed\" patches), and weak test suites (12.5%–22% of successful patches were logically wrong or incomplete due to insufficient tests).\n\nThe SWE-bench project is open source and available on GitHub, with active development and issue tracking throughout 2025."],"durationSeconds":7.3084749680000245}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018VuS1ipe9XF52LWQmkJkcd","type":"tool_result","content":"# STING Study: Test Mutation and Regression Results\n\n## Key Findings on Test Failures and Mutations\n\n**Surviving Variants (RQ1):**\n- \"77% of instances contain at least one surviving variant\" that passes original tests despite being semantically incorrect\n- 385 of 500 instances (77.0%) have surviving variants\n- Operator-based mutation: 209 surviving variants across 50 instances (10%)\n- LLM-based mutation: 1,915 surviving variants across 380 instances (76%)\n\n**Test Adequacy Metrics:**\nPredicate and Boolean Logic mutations dominated failures:\n- condfalse: 55 surviving variants\n- condtrue: 16 surviving variants  \n- eqflip: 13 surviving variants\n- Conditional logic accounts for 52.2% of operator-based and 54.3% of LLM-based survivors\n\n**Augmented Test Effectiveness (RQ2):**\n- Line coverage increased from 40.8% to 51.6% (+10.8 percentage points)\n- Branch coverage increased from 41.7% to 51.2% (+9.5 percentage points)\n- Generated tests contain 5.18 assertions per suite vs. 2.31 for developer tests (+124.2%)\n\n**Impact on Patch Evaluation (RQ3):**\nAugmented tests killed previously accepted patches:\n- 329 total patches rejected across top-10 agents\n- Resolved rates dropped 4.2% to 9.0%\n- Individual agents: 21-45 patches killed per STING Study: Test Mutation and Regression Results\n\n## Key Findings on Test Failures and Mutations\n\n**Surviving Variants (RQ1):**\n- \"77% of instances contain at least one surviving variant\" that passes original tests despite being semantically incorrect\n- 385 of 500 instances (77.0%) have surviving variants\n- Operator-based mutation: 209 surviving variants across 50 instances (10%)\n- LLM-based mutation: 1,915 surviving variants across 380 instances (76%)\n\n**Test Adequacy Metrics:**\nPredicate and Boolean Logic mutations dominated failures:\n- condfalse: 55 surviving variants\n- condtrue: 16 surviving variants  \n- eqflip: 13 surviving variants\n- Conditional logic accounts for 52.2% of operator-based and 54.3% of LLM-based survivors\n\n**Augmented Test Effectiveness (RQ2):**\n- Line coverage increased from 40.8% to 51.6% (+10.8 percentage points)\n- Branch coverage increased from 41.7% to 51.2% (+9.5 percentage points)\n- Generated tests contain 5.18 assertions per suite vs. 2.31 for developer tests (+124.2%)\n\n**Impact on Patch Evaluation (RQ3):**\nAugmented tests killed previously accepted patches:\n- 329 total patches rejected across top-10 agents\n- {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01RqCMQkxjhxAs2s1LayjH4S","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"\"incorrect patch\" \"number of tests\" failed regression SWE-bench mean median percentage broken\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Are “Solved Issues” in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2503.15223v1\"},{\"title\":\"[2503.15223] Are \\\"Solved Issues\\\" in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15223\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench: A Comprehensive Review of its Fundamentals, Methodology, Impact, and Future Directions\",\"url\":\"https://atoms.dev/insights/swe-bench-a-comprehensive-review-of-its-fundamentals-methodology-impact-and-future-directions/6c3cb9820d3b44e69862f7b064c1fd1e\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-Bench Won’t Save You When Production Burns | by Freedom Preetham | The Simulacrum | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/the-simulacrum/swe-bench-wont-save-you-when-production-burns-a819d0de38e4\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench Verified Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified\"},{\"title\":\"What skills does SWE-bench Verified evaluate? | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/blog/what-skills-does-swe-bench-verified-evaluate\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench 500: Benchmark for Automated Patch Repair\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-500\"},{\"title\":\"Are ``Solved Issues'' in SWE-bench Really ...\",\"url\":\"https://software-lab.org/publications/icse2026_SWE-bench-correctness.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"SWE-bench Verified Issues Benchmark\",\"url\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-issues\"},{\"title\":\"Dissecting the SWE-Bench Leaderboards: Profiling Submitters and Architectures of LLM- and Agent-Based Repair Systems\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.17208v2\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here are the key findings regarding incorrect patches and failed regressions in SWE-bench:\n\n## Incorrect Patch Statistics\n\nAmong plausible patches generated by three state-of-the-art tools, 7.2% to 8.4% are functionally incorrect when subjected to all developer tests. This translates to an absolute drop of 3.8% to 5.2% in reported resolution rates.\n\n## Regression Detection and Test Coverage\n\n7.8% of all patches count as correct while failing the developer-written test suite. Additionally, 29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than the ground truth patches. Manual inspection shows that 28.6% of behaviorally divergent patches are certainly incorrect.\n\n## Impact on Resolution Rates\n\nCombined, the different weaknesses lead to an inflation of reported resolution rates by 6.2 absolute percent points.\n\n## Weak Test Oracles\n\nAbout 31% of instances with passing patches rely on insufficiently robust test suites that fail to catch incomplete or incorrect modifications, allowing \"plausible\" but semantically wrong patches to pass the evaluation harness.\n\n## Patch Validation Flaws\n\nThe test suite typically runs only those test files modified in the PR, not all available tests, leading to an estimated overstatement of passing rates by 4–7% (absolute) due to missed regression cases.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"12b663c2-28b1-4a6e-8676-5700c43ad1a5","uuid":"4e8edbf2-0840-436a-80ee-d8f2f990bf91","timestamp":"2026-05-11T19:49:19.510Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"\"incorrect patch\" \"number of tests\" failed regression SWE-bench mean median percentage broken","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01QHSwj8xTGKgetRCwFVE8W4","content":[{"title":"Are “Solved Issues” in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2503.15223v1"},{"title":"[2503.15223] Are \"Solved Issues\" in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15223"},{"title":"SWE-bench: A Comprehensive Review of its Fundamentals, Methodology, Impact, and Future Directions","url":"https://atoms.dev/insights/swe-bench-a-comprehensive-review-of-its-fundamentals-methodology-impact-and-future-directions/6c3cb9820d3b44e69862f7b064c1fd1e"},{"title":"SWE-Bench Won’t Save You When Production Burns | by Freedom Preetham | The Simulacrum | Medium","url":"https://medium.com/the-simulacrum/swe-bench-wont-save-you-when-production-burns-a819d0de38e4"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified"},{"title":"What skills does SWE-bench Verified evaluate? | Epoch AI","url":"https://epoch.ai/blog/what-skills-does-swe-bench-verified-evaluate"},{"title":"SWE-bench 500: Benchmark for Automated Patch Repair","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-500"},{"title":"Are ``Solved Issues'' in SWE-bench Really ...","url":"https://software-lab.org/publications/icse2026_SWE-bench-correctness.pdf"},{"title":"SWE-bench Verified Issues Benchmark","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/swe-bench-verified-issues"},{"title":"Dissecting the SWE-Bench Leaderboards: Profiling Submitters and Architectures of LLM- and Agent-Based Repair Systems","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.17208v2"}]},"Based on the web search results, here are the key findings regarding incorrect patches and failed regressions in SWE-bench:\n\n## Incorrect Patch Statistics\n\nAmong plausible patches generated by three state-of-the-art tools, 7.2% to 8.4% are functionally incorrect when subjected to all developer tests. This translates to an absolute drop of 3.8% to 5.2% in reported resolution rates.\n\n## Regression Detection and Test Coverage\n\n7.8% of all patches count as correct while failing the developer-written test suite. Additionally, 29.6% of plausible patches induce different behavior than the ground truth patches. Manual inspection shows that 28.6% of behaviorally divergent patches are certainly incorrect.\n\n## Impact on Resolution Rates\n\nCombined, the different weaknesses lead to an inflation of reported resolution rates by 6.2 absolute percent points.\n\n## Weak Test Oracles\n\nAbout 31% of instances with passing patches rely on insufficiently robust test suites that fail to catch incomplete or incorrect modifications, allowing \"plausible\" but semantically wrong patches to pass the evaluation harness.\n\n## Patch Validation Flaws\n\nThe test suite typically runs only those test files modified in the PR, not all available tests, leading to an estimated overstatement of passing rates by 4–7% (absolute) due to missed regression cases."],"durationSeconds":7.417716365}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_015oZh3rrXp99iiyLM8u3Dhq","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I found a very important paper: the STING paper (arxiv:2604.01518) which specifically measures how many patches are killed by augmented tests. Key finding: \"329 total patches rejected across top-10 agents, resolved rates dropped 4.2% to 9.0%.\"\n\nBut I still don't have the specific per-test P2P regression rate. Let me try the full PDF of the ICSE 2026 paper (2503.15223) which is available at software-lab.org.\n\nLet me also try a different search approach - looking for the actual per-test data.\n \nNow I'm working with what I can extract from the literature: the SWE-bench test landscape shows a mean of 120.3 P2P tests per instance and 3.0 F2P tests, while the ICSE 2026 paper indicates that 7.8% of patches pass the P2P test set but fail the full developer test suite, suggesting the P2P tests are only a subset of the complete testing landscape.\n\nThe STING paper reveals that 77% of instances have mutations surviving the original test suite, with augmented tests catching an additional 4.2-9.0% of incorrect patches, and running the full test suite rather than just test_patch tests catches another 4-7% of failures. This points to a fundamental weakness in test coverage where many incorrect patches slip through.\n\nThe critical realization is that SWE-bench only executes the tests in test_patch, which means it's missing a significant portion of the actual test coverage that developers would run.\n\nWithin that limited test subset, incorrect patches fall into distinct categories: those that fail to fix the bug but don't break existing tests (the most common type), those that fix the bug but introduce regressions, and those that both fail to fix and break tests. The majority of incorrect patches in SWE-bench are the \"no-op\" variety that simply miss the bug without triggering any P2P test failures. But the real question is what happens when incorrect patches do trigger regressions—how often does that actually occur?\n\nLooking at this practically, a patch is marked resolved only if it passes all F2P and P2P tests, so unresolved patches can fail in various combinations: F2P only, P2P only, both, or fail to apply entirely. Most incorrect patches from LLM agents are semantically flawed but syntactically valid—they modify the wrong code or use incorrect logic—and these typically don't break P2P tests because they're isolated to the target area and the modifications tend to be incomplete rather than actively destructive.\n\nThat said, some patches do cause regressions when they alter shared utilities, introduce syntax errors, or modify APIs that other tests depend on. Based on what I've found in the literature, most LLM-generated incorrect patches have zero P2P regression, and those that do break tests only affect a small fraction.
